## 4. Confirmed code issues in the new hardening

File: `tests/extra_source_and_tests/extra_src/shared/electromagnetic_ophelie/electromagnetic_ophelie_french_reduced_geometry.h`.

### 4.1 The original fix is correct, but invalid inputs still pass

The former `%lf` with float pointers is undefined behavior and can overwrite memory; it is more serious than ordinary numerical rounding. The new double-temporary fix removes that specific type mismatch.

However, the parser silently ignores failed conversion, accepts trailing garbage, and accepts NaN. The new guard checks `dz > tol`, which does not reject NaN.

I compiled the actual current parser/refresh/validation function bodies with a minimal parameter stub and float Real. Results:

| Input | Result |
|---|---|
| `--glass-center=0,0,0.105` | Accepted correctly |
| `--glass-center=0,0,nan` | Accepted; center.z and automatically derived coil z become NaN |
| `--glass-center=nan,0,0.105` | Accepted with invalid x coordinate |
| `--glass-center=broken` | Accepted; previous/default center silently retained |
| `--glass-center=0,0,0.105garbage` | Accepted |

These are reproduced input-validation defects, not a claim that the archived valid commands used NaN.

Required bounded patch:

- Require exactly three finite numeric coordinates and complete consumption of the value string, allowing only explicitly permitted trailing whitespace.
- Reject conversion failure with the offending option/value; never silently keep defaults for a requested malformed option.
- Check finiteness after conversion to Real, including overflow from a finite double into float infinity.
- Validate finite positive dimensions/dp/radius, finite coil bounds and sensible loop/segment counts before geometry construction. Avoid converting a negative loop count through atoi into a huge size_t.
- Add focused tests for the original float build, malformed/NaN/Inf/overflow inputs and trailing garbage. Compile the touched parser with format warnings enabled.

### 4.2 The frame guard confuses a specific benchmark convention with general geometric consistency

`validateFrenchReducedGlassCoilFrame` requires `center.z == half_height` within tolerance, irrespective of whether the coil positions are absolute or automatically derived from that same center.

A translated glass with center.z=.205 and half-height=.105 is rejected even when `auto_coil_z=true` has consistently translated its coil stack. That is a valid world-frame geometry, although it is outside the current bottom-at-zero benchmark convention.

The default benchmark remains unaffected; this is a restriction/regression in the general shared helper.

Required design:

- Make bottom-at-zero an explicit contract of the canonical reference/export profile, rather than a universal physical requirement.
- In general geometry, validate the declared relative melt/coil positions and coordinate transform.
- For CSV normalization, transform coil locations and particle coordinates through the same frame; also retain world coordinates.
- Never silently move geometry to satisfy an assertion.
- Test a rigid translation of glass and coils together: fields at corresponding relative points must remain the same. Deliberately translating only the melt should change the physical problem and be detected against the reference manifest.

The new guard catches the reported old failure in its canonical profile, but passing it alone does not certify all source alignment.

## 5. EM power narrative: correct the arithmetic and separate causes

### 5.1 Definite numerical interpretation error

`power_residual.json` says SPH is “within ~1% of P0_particles.” Its own values show:

```text
P0_continuum       = 2.275390477 W
P0_particles_soft  = 2.324753053 W
P_recon_coil_only  = 2.259731616 W
P_GetDP_conducting = 2.128921116 W
P_SPH_oneway      = 2.341618040 W
```

Therefore:

| Comparison | Relative difference |
|---|---:|
| P0_particles_soft / P0_continuum − 1 | **+2.1694%** |
| P_recon / P0_particles_soft − 1 | **−2.7969%** |
| P_recon / P0_continuum − 1 | **−0.6882%** |
| P_GetDP_conducting / P0_continuum − 1 | **−6.4371%** |
| P_recon / P_GetDP_conducting − 1 | **+6.1445%** |
| P_oneway / P_GetDP_conducting − 1 | **+9.9908%** |

The sub-1% continuum agreement includes cancellation between the positive support/source-quadrature difference and the negative reconstruction difference. It is not evidence that each step is accurate to 1%.

The support term also compares a softened particle reference with the ideal continuum reference; separate softening and quadrature if attributing it quantitatively. The particle volume is 0.041688 m³, approximately **1.1025% above** nominal 0.0412334 m³. This further reinforces the need to compare on actual support rather than assuming equal integration measures.

Correct the interpretation string in both JSON locations and the corresponding Doc63 prose. Preserve the numerical measurements.

### 5.2 The signed budget is useful, but not a physical attribution by itself

The difference P_recon−P_GetDP can be written exactly from the supplied numbers:

```text
P_recon − P0_particles_soft       = −0.06502144 W
P0_particles_soft − P0_continuum  = +0.04936258 W
P0_continuum − P_GetDP_conducting = +0.14646936 W
------------------------------------------------
P_recon − P_GetDP_conducting      = +0.13081050 W
```

The last term is numerically dominant. It combines conducting feedback, finite-domain effects, FEM discretization/integration and any remaining source-definition differences. It has not been experimentally decomposed in this pack.

**Accepted:** comparing no-feedback SPH with conducting FEM is a mode mismatch, so retuning Js to make those two numbers equal would be wrong.

**Not yet accepted as closed:** attributing essentially the entire remaining term to physical demagnetization. A matched FEM prescribed-field baseline is missing.

### 5.3 One-way feedback moves farther from FEM in power

One-way feedback increases SPH power by 3.6237%, while its ratio to conducting GetDP moves from 1.0614 to 1.0999 and full masked absolute Q L2 from 0.10386 to 0.12214.

This does not by itself prove a sign error; a one-way update is not a converged self-consistent solution. But improved shell fraction does not demonstrate improved overall EM accuracy. Preserve complex phase conventions and keep this branch experimental until a matched reduced test explains its response.

### 5.4 Do not make the midplane the primary gate for the whole thermal source

Midplane correlation≈0.984 is encouraging, but the midplane normalized-shape L2 is **0.1183**, not the full-domain absolute value **0.1039**. These are different metrics.

For temperature/mixing reproduction, retain full-domain and axial Q profiles, integrated regions and consistently filtered comparisons. The current pack does not include regenerated normalized full-domain shape metrics, raw fields or the current comparator, so it cannot close those checks independently.

The old approximately 43% shape discrepancy should no longer dominate planning; the remaining roughly 10–12% discrepancies deserve bounded numerical qualification rather than a speculative solver rewrite.

## 6. F02 cadence: engineering acceptance is justified

The CSVs independently confirm:

| Quantity | 20/20 | 100/100 |
|---|---:|---:|
| CSV data rows | 88 | 17 |
| Final CSV sample time [s] | 5.9704 | 5.76471 |
| Reported simulation end time [s] | 6.000131 | 6.000372 |
| Acoustic steps | 16001 | 16000 |
| Applied source energy [J] | 355015.840834 | 355035.843562 |
| Boundary energy [J] | 405609.214622 | 405669.204226 |
| Endpoint residual / throughput | 0.0878% | 0.0711% |
| Endpoint residual / predicted net | 0.7038% | 0.5695% |

Relative differences in source and boundary energy are approximately 0.00563% and 0.01479%. The small endpoint-time difference also contributes; comparing average energy rates gives approximately 0.00162% and 0.01077% differences. That normalization is descriptive, not a substitute for an identical trajectory.

**Allowed wording:** “The repaired production boundary ledger is engineering-accepted on the exercised 6 s constant-property path, with small sensitivity under a genuine fivefold diagnostic-cadence change.”

Do not require bitwise-identical full GPU trajectories. Do not block unrelated development on the fixed-state shadow test. Keep that test as a small regression aid, ideally combined with the F07 operator-energy audit, if a stronger invariant claim is needed.

What is not established: identical physical trajectories, all logging/backend configurations, or that diagnostic reads can never mutate state. The current source needed to inspect every observer path is omitted.

## 7. Thermal/flow observations that should not be waved away

### 7.1 Endpoint energy closure is good; sampled histories also remain small

For the 88-row run, the maximum sampled cumulative residual is approximately **0.2218% of throughput** and **1.8781% of net storage**, near t=.155662 s. These are higher than endpoint values but remain modest. No nonfinite values occur in either supplied energy CSV.

The instantaneous `dE_dt` residual and cumulative ledger residual measure different things; finite-difference diagnostic cadence can change the former without changing the integrated ledger.

The CSV precision is limited: reconstructing H−EQ+EB from printed large energies can differ from the directly printed residual by fractions of a joule. Use sufficient precision in future audit exports; do not misclassify this as an operator error.

Doc64 says “both much less than 1% gate,” while its summary and Doc65 use **energy_tol=.02**, i.e. 2%. Correct the wording or record the actual runtime threshold from the current binary. No stricter gate should be inferred from prose alone.

### 7.2 Separate stirring-velocity comparability question; no established link to EM repair

The following are from the **mechanical stirring case**, not the natural-convection case. They are end-of-run `U_rms` values, not averages over the entire revolution:

```text
Doc56: 0.133376 m/s
Doc64: 0.067342 m/s
Doc65: 0.074911 m/s
```

Doc56→64 decreases by approximately **49.5%**; Doc64→65 differs by approximately **11.24%**. U_theta also changes strongly between Docs56/64. In the previously supplied stirring source, the final `U_rms` is computed from `spatial_end` at the final state; this pack omits the current stirring source, so confirm that its definition has not changed.

**These numbers do not show that correcting EM changed the stirring speed.** The diagnosed `%lf` bug arose in reduced EM diagnostics invoked with `--glass-center=`; the supplied production stirring command instead loads an STL melt and does not show that option. Whether it shared any affected input path must be checked, not assumed. Doc56/64 report very similar mean temperatures (1472.604858/1472.605835 K) and reconstructed EM powers (59830.96/59848.78 W). The difference in `U_rms` is therefore not currently attributable to the EM frame repair, and any general statement about thermal-field effects on speed would require additional evidence.

Different binaries/inputs, definitions, endpoint sampling, acoustic oscillations and physical trajectory divergence must be separated before explaining the velocity values. Do not assert ordinary GPU scatter or turbulent chaos without evidence. This is not a confirmed hydrodynamic defect. End-state differences also do not establish a comparable difference in window-averaged mixing. They limit **quantitative stirring/mixing claims**, not the acceptance of the repaired EM diagnostic or the F02 energy ledger.

Follow-up when preparing a stirring/mixing comparison (independent of §9's EM measurement):

- First compare Doc56/64/65 build identities, reloads, stirring geometry, rotor motion, viscosity and statistics definition. Establish whether the EM diagnostic's `--glass-center=` path is even used by production stirring.
- If these inputs and definitions match, use an identical binary/reload and one short repeated 20/20 run; compare a common physical-time window of `U_rms`, `U_theta`, rotor torque and circulation, rather than end-state samples only.
- Compare same-cadence repeats with the 20/20-to-100/100 variation. Only if a reproducible velocity change appears should a controlled fixed-Q versus changed-Q run test a possible thermal/EM influence.
- Do not run a longer simulation solely to investigate an unverified causal association.

### 7.3 A one-revolution energy pass is not thermal equilibrium or mixing validation

At the endpoint, mean temperature is only about 0.394 K below 1473 K, while T_std≈4.23 K. One revolution can exercise coupling and bookkeeping without establishing stationary thermal structure or mature mixing.

Under fixed-current control, P_joule≈59849 W being close to the initial 60 kW target is not independently a validation of field accuracy, especially after initial current calibration. Keep operating-point control and solver verification distinct.

## 8. F07: what is blocked, and what is not

Stable total Mass and a 1.8% difference from rho0*V do not demonstrate mass loss. If volume follows m/rho and density differs from rho0, this difference may be expected.

However, even sum(rho*V) differs from Mass by approximately **0.0756%** in Doc64 and **0.0451%** in Doc65. If the framework promises V=m/rho at the sampling stage, test that identity particlewise and synchronize all fields at the same algorithmic stage. The mismatch could reflect staggered updates/diagnostic timing or a different convention; the pack does not establish its cause. “Small” is not an explanation.

The current energy CSVs do not include rho*V or rho extrema, so the endpoint reports cannot show the full history of this identity.

**Does not block:** static EM work, limited explicitly provisional coupling tests, parser repairs, or flow-observation diagnostics.

**Does block:** a claimed physically conservative thermal baseline and quantitative literature temperature prediction until the storage/increment convention is resolved.

Do a short operator audit around hydro/density updates, source, diffusion, BC and clamp. Record mass-based enthalpy and the existing rho0*V-based storage alongside actual applied energy. Choose a convention consistent with the model and make the evolution equations, storage diagnostic and power integration agree. Do not change only the diagnostic H to make a residual look better.

## 9. One next EM measurement: passive heating from FEM source-only A

Use the existing aligned seven-loop geometry, same FEM mesh/domain and I=1 A. Preserve the independently validated source definitions.

1. Solve source-only A with induced-current feedback suppressed.
2. Recover the **physical Aphi** using the installed template's documented field transformation; retain signed complex output and an A/B flux consistency check.
3. Postprocess a passive conductivity `sigma_eval=16 S/m`:

```text
Q_passive = 0.5 * sigma_eval * omega^2 * |Aphi_coil_only|^2
P_FEM_passive = integral_glass Q_passive * 2*pi*r dr dz
```

4. Compare `P_FEM_passive` with P0_continuum and with the conducting FEM power on exactly the same geometry/integration contract.
5. Sample the same source-only FEM A at actual SPH positions if needed to make the prescribed-field reconstruction comparison share support.

**Important:** GetDP's existing source-only run uses sigma≈1e-12. Its actual physical Joule loss is nearly zero. Merely checking that zero loss does not answer the mode-mismatch question. Passive sigma_eval belongs only to postprocessing; it must not be reintroduced into the source-only PDE.

Interpretation:

- P_FEM_passive≈P0 while P_FEM_conducting is lower: supports attributing that difference to induced feedback within this FEM model, subject to its numerical accuracy.
- P_FEM_passive is already appreciably below P0: quantify FEM domain/source/discretization effects before calling the entire deficit demagnetization.
- On matching particle support, a remaining P_recon deficit relative to prescribed A isolates SPH reconstruction/boundary/discretization effects.

This is the priority measurement; no 1000 s run or full-production Picard is required. Only add a targeted mesh/domain refinement if its result is needed to resolve the measured residual.

## 10. Development order toward the French papers

### Next bounded cycle

1. Fix malformed/nonfinite CLI handling and scope the canonical frame guard correctly.
2. Correct the 1% versus 2.8% power interpretation, gate-threshold prose and causal wording.
3. Run the matched FEM passive-heating measurement in §9.
4. In parallel as a separate task, document thermal mass/volume convention and run a short operator-energy audit; include a fixed-state boundary ledger check if convenient.
5. When preparing quantitative stirring/mixing results, first reconcile existing run identities and the end-state velocity definition; run a short matched repeat and time-window comparison only if this cannot be resolved from the archived inputs. This is independent of the EM frame repair and is not a prerequisite for §9.

### Following cycle, once these contracts are adequate

- Check the relevant EM/spatial resolution and thermal timestep sensitivity on a small controlled setup; a single dp=.02 reduced diagnostic is not a convergence result.
- Bridge the reduced reference to the actual stirring configuration. Confirm actual production geometry, particle/reload support, conductivity law/value, frequency, coil settings, softening, Q deposition and current calibration. Earlier production runs used a different conductivity/particle resolution from the sigma=16, dp=.02 EM benchmark; record current values rather than assuming transfer of validation.
- Select the paper observables and averaging intervals: absorbed power/source distribution, radial/axial temperature structure, flow circulation, torque/power or mixing quantities as applicable. Resolve material/thermal-boundary provenance in parallel.
- A 5-revolution run can then answer a defined question about developing circulation, energy drift or thermal transients. It is not an automatic acceptance gate and does not establish stationarity merely by reaching five revolutions.
- Natural convection should resume with the existing controlled force/buoyancy audit, rather than jumping to expensive refinement. Stirring smoke does not validate that branch.

Do not let “deferred by policy” become a permanent prohibition. Advance to longer physical time when inputs, conservation and numerical sensitivity support the intended observable. Continue to avoid skull/current retuning to hide errors and premature full-production induced-field iteration.

## 11. Explicit answers to the discussion brief

1. **EM residual closure:** accept that no-feedback versus conducting modes differ and Js must not be tuned to force equality. Do not yet close the causal attribution of all ~6% to feedback. Midplane shape is a partial check, not the main acceptance criterion for the whole heating field.
2. **F02 status:** yes, engineering-accepted within the exercised path/horizon. The shadow ledger can remain a separate OPEN regression item; it need not block other development.
3. **Coupled versus literature:** yes, 6 s smoke is not literature readiness. A 5-rev run is optional only with a defined transient question. The matched EM and thermal-energy audits are immediate priorities; stirring-velocity comparability is a separate check before quantitative flow claims.
4. **F07 mass:** it blocks physical thermal-energy/literature claims, not all engineering or static EM work. Do not declare the issue solved from stable total mass alone.
5. **Stop/go:** go. The next EM measurement is passive heating from the source-only FEM A field. Do not launch another broad speculative model rewrite.

## 12. Next review pack and limits of closure

Provide the minimal current sources/evidence needed to certify outstanding items:

- parser/frame regression results and current geometry header;
- source-only FEM passive/conducting power table with raw outputs and actual template;
- current SPH diagnostic/reconstruction and comparison/export scripts, runtime frame/source manifest, and representative/raw fields needed for common-support verification;
- current stirring thermal/gate code if claiming F01/F03/F05/F07 closure;
- frozen build/input identities and windowed flow repeatability metrics;
- operator-energy and same-state mass/rho/volume audit;
- a final endpoint CSV/JSON record independent of ordinary logging cadence.

The current energy CSVs stop before the reported final endpoints. Their histories are useful, but endpoint gate values presently come only from the supplied summary JSON. Store an explicit final record with adequate precision so these can be reconstructed without relying on a summary generator.

Prior Doc60 exporter metadata/component-substitution concerns are not confirmed fixed by this pack because the relevant updated scripts are absent. Keep their status unverified; do not assert either that the old defects persist or that they are closed without the current code.

# File: `67_CLI_HARDEN_AND_FEM_PASSIVE_HEATING_2026-09-27.md`

# Doc 67 — CLI harden, corrected power narrative, FEM passive heating

Date: 2026-09-27  
Parent: Doc 66 (post-Doc65 GPT review)  
Artifacts: `reference/em_rz_getdp/work/doc67_passive_heating/`, updated `work/doc63_baseline/power_residual.json`

## 1. Decision

| Item | Status |
|---|---|
| Strict CLI parse (finite, full consumption, no silent default) | **done** |
| Canonical bottom-at-zero scoped via `require_canonical_bottom_at_zero` | **done** (defaults on reduced EM; cleared for stirring STL) |
| Doc63 “~1% of P0_particles” wording | **corrected** (~2.8% below particles; ~0.7% below continuum) |
| Doc64 “≪1% gate” | **corrected** (runtime tol = **2%**) |
| FEM passive heating from source-only A (`σ_eval` postprocess only) | **done** |
| Attribute entire ~6% conducting deficit to demagnetization | **rejected** — see §3 |
| Retune Js / 1000 s / Picard-on / skull | **still deferred** |

## 2. Code (Doc 66 §4)

File: `electromagnetic_ophelie_french_reduced_geometry.h`

- `--glass-center=` and other numeric options use `strtod`/`strtol` with **full-string** consumption; NaN/Inf/trailing garbage/`broken` → FATAL (never keep prior center).
- Positive counts reject negatives (no `atoi`→`size_t` wrap).
- `validateFrenchReducedFiniteGeometry` before mesh construction.
- `require_canonical_bottom_at_zero`: set in `applyFrenchReducedDefaults`; cleared in `syncFrenchStirringGlassToFrenchParams`. Translated melt + `auto_coil_z` is allowed when the flag is clear.
- Contract tests: `reference/em_rz_getdp/test_doc67_glass_center_parse.cpp` (all passed).

## 3. FEM passive heating (Doc 66 §9)

Runner: `run_doc67_passive_heating.sh`  
Postprocess: `passive_heating_from_az.py`  
Same aligned 7-loop mesh; I=1 A; `Flag_coil_only=1` then conducting.

| Quantity | W | /P0 − 1 |
|---|---:|---:|
| P0 continuum | 2.27539 | 0 |
| **P_FEM_passive** (coil-only A, σ_eval=16) | **2.20622** | **−3.04%** |
| P_FEM_conducting | 2.12892 | −6.44% |
| P_SPH coil-only recon | 2.25973 | −0.69% |
| Coil-only PDE Joule (σ~1e−12) | ~0 | — |

Decomposition of `P0 − P_conducting = 0.1465 W` on the **baseline bounded domain** (`R_ext=1.5 m`, `z_pad=0.6 m`):

| Term | W | share of (P0−P_cond) |
|---|---:|---:|
| `P0 − P_passive` (FEM truncation/source/discretization/postprocess vs continuum P0) | **0.0692** | **~47%** (Doc69: 52.8% of same split using archived passive) |
| `P_passive − P_conducting` (induced feedback **within** this FEM solve) | **0.0773** | **~53%** |

Doc69 amendment: these percentages describe **this finite domain only**. On the enlarged domain (`R=2.0`, `z_pad=0.8`) the within-FEM mode gap stays ~0.079 W while `P0−P_passive` shrinks, so the feedback share of `(P0−P_cond)` rises to **~69%** — do **not** treat “half/half” as an infinite-domain physical split. `P0−P_passive` mixes far-field BC, source representation, FE discretization and quadrature; it is not purely “FEM truncation.”

**Reading:**

1. Passive heating is **already ~3% below P0** on this mesh/domain — do **not** call the full ~6.4% conducting deficit “demagnetization.”
2. Feedback inside the FEM model explains about **half** of `(P0−P_conducting)` **on the baseline domain**; the other half is present even without induced currents. Domain enlargement changes that arithmetic split (Doc 68/69).
3. SPH coil-only sits ~2.4% above FEM passive (`P_SPH/P_passive≈1.024`) and ~0.7% below P0 — reconstruction/support effects remain, but are not the old frame bug.
4. Coil-only physical Joule ~0 confirms `σ_eval` stayed out of the PDE.

Midplane |Aφ| samples from coil-only `az.pos` are slightly below Doc60 independent filament values (e.g. r≈0.24: 2.10e−6 vs 2.16e−6), consistent with a mild FEM source/domain soft under-prediction.

## 4. Corrected Doc63 arithmetic (preserved measurements)

| Compare | Rel. difference |
|---|---:|
| P0_particles / P0 − 1 | **+2.17%** |
| P_recon / P0_particles − 1 | **−2.80%** |
| P_recon / P0 − 1 | **−0.69%** |
| P_conducting / P0 − 1 | **−6.44%** |

JSON: `work/doc63_baseline/power_residual.json` (interpretation rewritten) and `work/doc67_passive_heating/power_budget_corrected.json`.

## 5. What this does / does not close

**Closes for planning:** Js retune to match conducting GetDP remains wrong; causal claim that “~6% = feedback only” is unsupported; CLI NaN/garbage silent accept is fixed; frame guard is not a universal translated-geometry veto.

**Still OPEN:** mesh/domain refinement to shrink (P0−P_passive); common-support SPH↔FEM prescribed-A compare; F07 storage convention; stirring U_rms identity/repeatability; frozen-particle F02 shadow ledger (optional).

## 6. Suggested next (Doc 66 order, remaining)

1. Short F07 operator-energy / mass–ρ–V identity audit (parallel; blocks literature thermal claims only).
2. Optional: one mesh/domain refinement of the passive/conducting pair if needed to tighten the FEM-vs-P0 gap.
3. When claiming stirring/mixing numbers: reconcile Doc56/64/65 identities and window-average velocities — independent of this EM pack.

# File: `68_F07_FLOW_AND_FEM_REFINE_2026-09-28.md`

# Doc 68 — F07 mass/ρ/V audit, flow-window comparability, FEM refine

Date: 2026-09-28  
Parent: Doc 66 §7–10 / Doc 67  
Artifacts:
- `reference/stirring_stage3_r0/doc68_f07_flow_audit/`
- `reference/em_rz_getdp/work/doc68_fem_refine/`

## 1. Decision

| Item | Status |
|---|---|
| Particlewise `Mass−ρV` + parallel `H_m=Σ m cp ΔT` (diagnostic) | **done** (CSV schema **5**; production ledger still `H=Σ ρ0 cp V ΔT`) |
| 2 s F07 smoke with rebuilt binary | **passed** (`validation_passed=1`) |
| Archive U_rms Doc56/64/65 window compare | **done** — endpoint ≠ mixing metric |
| FEM passive/conducting mesh + domain sensitivity | **done** |
| Close literature thermal energy / switch production H to mass | **not done** — convention documented; needs matched Q/BC before any switch |
| 1000 s / Picard / skull / paper T | **still deferred** |

## 2. Code / tooling

- `hostThermalMassVolumeAudit`: `max_abs` / `rms` / `rel_l2` of particlewise `Mass−ρV`.
- `hostThermalEnergyConstantCpFromMass`: diagnostic only.
- Energy CSV schema 5 adds `sum_rho_vol`, ρ extrema/mean, Mass−ρV stats, `thermal_energy_mass`.
- Stirring final line prints the same F07 fields.
- Fixed Clang `[[noreturn]]` placement (`[[noreturn]] inline void`).
- Scripts: `run_doc68_f07_2s.sh`, `analyze_doc68_flow_mass_archive.py`, `run_doc68_fem_refine.sh`.

## 3. F07 results (2 s smoke)

| Quantity | Cadence CSV (last row, t≈1.996 s) | Endpoint summary (t=2.000 s) |
|---|---:|---:|
| `sum_mass` | 112.860 | 112.860296 |
| `sum_rho0_vol` | 110.657 | 110.657028 |
| `mass_vs_rho0V_rel` | ~1.95% | **1.95%** |
| `sum_rho_vol` | 112.860 | 112.299 |
| `mass_vs_rhoV_rel` | ~0 | **0.50%** |
| `rel_l2(Mass,ρV)` | **4.2e−8** | **0.0179** |
| `max\|Mass−ρV\|` | **4.5e−11** | **4.9e−5** |
| `H_rho0V` / `H_mass` | −16808 / −16901 | −16840 / −16933 |

**Reading:**

1. Mass is stable; `Mass ≠ ρ0·V` (~2%) is the WCSPH density/packing convention, **not** mass loss.
2. At diagnostic cadence samples, particlewise `Mass ≈ ρV` to machine precision after host sync — the identity holds in that synchronized state.
3. The endpoint summary shows a **staggered-state** mismatch (`rel_l2≈1.8%`). Do not treat endpoint `mass_vs_rhoV_rel` alone as a particlewise identity failure; synchronize Mass/Density/Vol before claiming a broken `V=m/ρ` contract.
4. Parallel `H_mass` tracks `H_rho0V` within ~90 J on this 2 s cool-down (~0.55% of \|H\|). Production residual still uses `H_rho0V`. **Do not** swap production H without matching Q/diffusion/BC measure.

**Blocks / does not block:** unchanged from Doc 66 — blocks claimed conservative thermal baseline / literature T; does not block static EM or provisional coupling.

## 4. Stirring U_rms comparability (archive)

| Metric | Doc56 | Doc64 | Doc65 | Doc56→64 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Endpoint summary `U_rms` | 0.1334 | 0.0673 | 0.0749 | **−49.5%** |
| CSV revolution mean | 0.1070 | 0.1069 | 0.1041 | **−0.15%** |
| CSV mean in t∈[1,5] s | (see JSON) | | | **−0.24%** |

Endpoint summary is a single late sample (post final EM update) and is **not** a mixing metric. Window means show no evidence that the EM frame repair changed stirring speed. Production stirring uses STL sync, not `--glass-center=`.

## 5. FEM refine (passive vs conducting)

Same I=1 A, σ_eval=16 postprocess; Doc67 integrator.

| Case | P_passive | /P0 | P_conducting | /P0 | P_pass−P_cond |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| baseline `lc_g=0.01`, `R_ext=1.5` | 2.206 | 0.970 | 2.129 | 0.936 | 0.077 |
| fine `lc_g=0.005`, `R_ext=1.5` | 2.209 | 0.971 | 2.131 | 0.937 | 0.077 |
| domain `lc_g=0.01`, `R_ext=2.0`, `z_pad=0.8` | **2.240** | **0.985** | **2.161** | **0.950** | 0.079 |

**Reading:**

1. Glass mesh refinement barely moves powers — not the main (P0−P_passive) driver.
2. Larger outer domain recovers ~1.5% of P0 on both passive and conducting; feedback gap stays ~0.077–0.079 W.
3. **Doc69 correction:** domain enlargement does **not** confirm a universal half/half split. On baseline (`R=1.5`, `z_pad=0.6`) feedback is ~53% of `(P0−P_cond)`; on enlarged (`R=2.0`, `z_pad=0.8`) it is ~69%, because `P0−P_passive` shrinks while the mode difference stays ~0.077–0.079 W. Still no Js retune.

## 6. Explicit next (optional)

1. If closing F07 for literature: one short frozen- or fixed-dt operator audit with **same-state** Mass/ρ/V sync around hydro, Q, diffusion, BC; then choose H convention and match increments.
2. Optional FEM: one more domain enlargement or far-field treatment if targeting <1% (P0−P_passive).
3. Quantitative stirring claims: use windowed `U_rms`/`U_θ` (and torque), not endpoint summary.

## 7. Pack

`docs/ophelie/packs/gpt_doc68_f07_flow_fem_2026-09-28.tar.gz`

<a id="69-post-doc68-audit-and-french-reproduction-next-steps-2026-09-28md"></a>

# File: `69_POST_DOC68_AUDIT_AND_FRENCH_REPRODUCTION_NEXT_STEPS_2026-09-28.md`

# Doc 69 — Review of Docs 67–68 and the next French-literature reproduction steps

Date: 2026-09-28  
Reviewed input: `gpt_doc62_to_68_discussion_2026-09-28.tar.gz` (56 extracted files)  
Predecessor: Doc 66, including its amended §7.2 on stirring velocity.  
Purpose: concrete engineering handoff to Cursor. No solver or uploaded source was modified by this review.

## 1. Executive decision

**Proceed.** The old melt/coil frame bug and invalid CLI inputs have credible fixes. The FEM passive experiment substantially improves the electromagnetic diagnosis. The 2 s thermal audit has identified a reproducible inconsistency between diagnostic samples and the final particle state. The archived stirring velocity averages remove the apparent 50% *revolution-mean* velocity difference; they do not explain the different final instantaneous samples.

**Current critical path to the papers:** establish an internally consistent thermal storage/source/boundary discretization, finish qualified spatial EM source comparison, then run controlled buoyancy and stirred thermal benchmarks at paper operating conditions. Do not launch a 1000 s run or tune current to conceal mismatched FEM/SPH models.

## 2. What the submitted evidence actually establishes

| Area | Accepted observation | Qualification |
|---|---|---|
| CLI/frame | `strtod`/`strtol`, full consumption and finite checks replace the unsafe `%lf`-into-float path. The canonical bottom-at-zero check is scoped and STL synchronization clears it. | `test_doc67_glass_center_parse.cpp` **copies** the parser instead of testing the compiled production header. Its negative-number assertion contains `|| true` and cannot fail. A focused test against production code is preferable. |
| Electromagnetic baseline | Old B≈0.84 and P≈0.694 comparisons from the bad frame are superseded. Same-current SPH coil-only power is 2.259732 W; the independent infinite-space filament reference is 2.275390 W. | Agreement with the continuum integral hides a −2.80% difference from the softened particle reference, whose power is 2.324753 W. Full-domain Q remains approximately 10% different by the archived metric and needs support-matched checking. |
| Passive FEM | Baseline source-only `A`, evaluated with σ=16 S/m *after* the solve, gives 2.206223 W. Conducting FEM on the same geometry gives 2.128921 W; difference 0.077302 W. | This is a useful **within-FEM mode difference** on this mesh and bounded domain, not a final physical demagnetization percentage relative to infinite space. `.pos` meshes were omitted from the pack, so their integration cannot be rerun from these uploaded bytes. |
| FEM sensitivity | Refining the glass mesh changes passive power 2.206223→2.208567 W (~0.11%). Increasing radius 1.5→2.0 m **and** axial padding 0.6→0.8 m changes it 2.206223→2.240305 W (~1.54%). | The domain experiment changes two bounds together. It establishes combined far-boundary sensitivity, not how much to assign individually to radial and axial truncation. |
| F07 mass audit | At sampled times, `Mass≈Density×VolumetricMeasure` particlewise (`rel_l2≈4.2e−8`). At t=2.000059 s the final summary instead shows `rel_l2≈0.01787`, mass 112.860296 kg and ΣρV 112.299067 kg. | “Staggered state” is a plausible mechanism, **not yet demonstrated**: all three fields are requested from host in the audit function. Identify the last operator that changes density, volume or particle indexing, and the exact position of the last CSV sample. |
| Thermal energy | At the last diagnostic sample near t=1.99631 s, `H_rho0V≈−16808 J`, `H_mass≈−16901 J`; final values are −16840.10 and −16933.29 J. Existing production residual uses rho0×V. | An approximately 93 J difference in absolute H does **not** directly imply a 93 J conservation error. Compare changes from a common initial state and match the weights in the heating/diffusion/BC operators. |
| Stirring | Doc56 and Doc64 CSV arithmetic means of `U_rms` are 0.107038 and 0.106881 m/s (−0.147%). On t∈[1,5] s their sample means differ −0.241%. | These are means of recorded samples, with no phase matching or continuous time integral. The full Doc56 raw spatial CSV is not included in this pack; its JSON summary cannot be independently recalculated here. No evidence ties an instantaneous stirring velocity change to the diagnostic EM frame repair. |
| F02 | The earlier genuine 20/20–100/100 twin is engineering-accepted on its tested 6 s constant-property path. | A 2 s F07 validation flag checks implementation thresholds; it does not certify energy conservation or paper agreement. |

### Important power-accounting correction

The exact split depends on the domain being compared:

| Same-current FEM domain | P0−Ppassive [W] | Ppassive−Pconducting [W] | Fraction of (P0−Pconducting) from the within-FEM mode difference |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| R=1.5 m, z padding=0.6 m | 0.069168 | 0.077302 | 52.8% |
| R=2.0 m, z padding=0.8 m | 0.035086 | 0.079047 | 69.3% |

Thus Doc67's “about half FEM truncation/source and half feedback” is an arithmetic description of the **baseline bounded solve only**. Doc68's statement that the same half/half split is *confirmed* by the domain enlargement is incorrect. The larger domain leaves a 1.54% passive deficit relative to P0; do not extrapolate the feedback fraction to the infinite-domain limit. The mode difference itself is fairly stable (0.0773→0.0790 W). `P0−Ppassive` mixes far-field boundary, source representation, FE discretization and postprocessing quadrature; it is not entirely “FEM truncation.”

## 3. Specific issues found in the submitted code and records

### 3.1 Published FEM metadata contradicts the solver inputs

`reference/em_rz_getdp/work/doc67_passive_heating/conducting/metadata.json` reports `domain_outer_radius_m=1.0`, `domain_z_pad_m=0.4`, `lc_glass_m=0.02`, `lc_air_m=0.06`, and `js0_phi=2*pi*I_peak/Area`. The supplied `.geo` defaults and Doc67 runner instead use R=1.5 m, padding=0.6 m, glass lc=0.010 m, air lc=0.050 m; the supplied `.pro` explicitly sets `Js=I_peak/AreaCoil`. Its `mesh_file` also points to a different location than the actual Doc67 mesh. These stale descriptive values could lead the next reviewer to reproduce the **wrong** problem. The archived power calculation agrees with the run logs, but the metadata **cannot certify its configuration**. Populate metadata from a run manifest of effective `gmsh -setnumber` and `getdp -setnumber` values and the actual mesh path; add a post-export assertion against the manifest. Correct existing Doc67 metadata or mark it superseded. Preserve both numerical power and its provenance.

### 3.2 Power quadrature needs a narrowly scoped cross-check

`passive_heating_from_az.py` filters triangles by centroid and uses the **square of the centroid average of three nodal A values** with centroid 2πr weighting. For a linear FE field, the integral of `r|A|²` is generally different from that centroid approximation; it may systematically understate passive power. The glass triangle count changes 1214→4900 while the reported volume remains the nominal 0.0412334 m³, supporting that it selects the intended region, but the omitted `.pos` prevents a direct inspection here. Implement either an exact/adequate Gaussian triangle rule using physical nodal A or a native GetDP passive integral with σ_eval applied only in postprocessing. On **the same** mesh compare: existing centroid power; improved quadrature/native power; physical conducting J-based `2πr` power; GetDP native power with the correct 2π convention. Export region IDs if possible instead of selecting solely by coordinates. Keep source-only PDE conductivity tiny.

### 3.3 Endpoint F07 state cannot yet be diagnosed by naming it “staggered”

`hostThermalMassVolumeAudit` synchronizes Volume, Mass and Density separately before reading them. The last CSV is at 1.99631 s, not the 2.000059 s endpoint; a hydro/acoustic update or field ordering can produce the discrepancy, but this is not proven by these two timestamps alone. Instrument **same-state** snapshots at (i) immediately after a known consistent sync, (ii) before/after the hydro density and volume updates, (iii) after heat source, diffusion and boundary application, and (iv) after the final timestep. Include step count, t, particle count, Σm, ΣρV, relL2(m−ρV), Σρ0V, H_m and H_rho0V in one sufficiently precise row per snapshot. Check that all fields refer to the same particle ordering. Verify whether `V=m/ρ` is meant to be re-established at that point in the method; do not forcibly synchronize or rewrite V to silence a genuine end-state inconsistency.

### 3.4 Velocity claim is narrower than “endpoint must never be used”

The Doc64 CSV ends at 5.9704 s and its final summary is near 6.0001 s. Its recent sampled `U_rms` ranges widely (approximately 0.078–0.117 m/s over the last several records). The dramatic Doc56/64 **summary** difference is real for those instantaneous recorded states, while the sampled means are nearly the same. An instantaneous end state can be useful if time and rotor phase are synchronized; it must not stand in for a revolution mean. Compare same physical intervals with time-weighted interpolation and, if practical, equal rotor phase; report `U_theta` and torque as well. Check binary revision, reload and operating flags first. This is an **independent stirring comparability task**, not an EM correction prerequisite.

### 3.5 CLI regression test limitation

The Doc67 test mirrors `parseStrictFiniteDouble`, `parseStrictCommaTripleDoubles` and the canonical check. As written, it could pass after the production code regresses; `check(!parseStrictFiniteDouble("-1", dp) || true, ...)` is unconditionally true. Replace that expression and add a thin compile-time test that calls the actual production parser/CLI with float `Real`, including invalid NaN/Inf, suffix, triple overflow, nonpositive loop counts and valid rigid translation. This is cleanup; it need not delay the next F07 measurement.

## 4. Cursor work order and acceptance evidence

### P0 — Correct reproducibility claims (small change)

1. Fix Doc67 metadata or tag the file superseded and produce an effective-input manifest. Amend Docs 67/68: “half/half” applies to the baseline finite domain; report 52.8% and 69.3% for the two measured domains. Clarify glass-mesh-only and combined-domain perturbations.
2. Keep the source-only/passive/conducting distinction in all new tables. Do not retune `Js` using a source-only SPH vs conducting FEM power difference.
3. Deliver the corrected manifest, changed code, exact commands, and outputs (include `.pos`/`.msh` or a small reproducible FE integral and checksums) in the next review pack.

### P1 — Close F07 on a bounded run (main thermal blocker)

1. Use a short repeat of the same 2 s constant-property case with diagnostic snapshots across the **same integration step**, plus one end-state snapshot at the last CSV stage. Record each operator's energy increment using both H conventions, `E_Q_applied`, and boundary energy with a common initial-state H. Include the actual heat-source, diffusion and BC weights; distinguish a diagnostic H mismatch from an energy-transfer error.
2. Find the precise operator/state transition causing endpoint `rel_l2(m,ρV)≈0.01787`; establish whether it is an intended operator split, an ordering bug or an actual V–ρ identity violation. Make any production change only after that localization.
3. Choose a conservation metric consistent with the implemented time-discrete source/diffusion/BC equations. Retest the previous F02 cadence variant only if code changes can affect its ledger. Acceptance: per-operator accounting closes to measured numerical tolerance **over steps and at endpoint**, with no unexplained state mismatch. Report tolerance and denominator; no paper-temperature claim yet.

### P2 — Qualify the FEM/SPH Q shape (bounded numerical work)

1. Cross-check passive FE integration as in §3.2. Once sound, do one additional sufficiently farther-boundary solve, **vary radial and axial boundaries separately** or use a validated far-field treatment. Check whether Ppassive and Pconducting stabilize within a declared tolerance; avoid declaring a specific physical feedback fraction before convergence.
2. Compare SPH and FEM **source-only** A/B and Q on common spatial support/weights, report full r–z maps and radial/axial power fractions. Keep the **conducting** FEM mode in a separate panel and identify SPH as coil-only or one-way, not a converged conductor. Track both amplitude and normalized shape and particle-volume discrepancy.
3. Do not make another domain step a gate for F07 or controlled thermal work. Retain the current ~10% Q-shape residual explicitly until the common-support analysis is complete.

### P3 — Return to the actual French-paper observables

1. Create a source-backed parameter/observable matrix for **each of the two French papers**: geometry, coil/forcing, current convention, material laws, ambient and wall conditions, time window, temperature points/profiles and velocity/mixing observable. Label missing values and calibrations; do not claim reproduction based only on numerical stability.
2. After P1, resume the natural-convection branch with a controlled buoyancy/force-direction and hydro baseline at short times. For mechanical stirring, use time/phase-aligned windows for `U_rms`, `U_theta` and torque, then temperature/source/BC convergence. Establish mesh, timestep, duration and measurement uncertainty before comparing with published numbers.
3. Extend physical time in stages only after short-run conservation and source/flow evidence pass. Treat skull/phase change, self-consistent Picard feedback and high-temperature constitutive uncertainty as explicit later model choices, not fixes for the present diagnostics.

## 5. What to send back in the next pack

One updated status note plus: effective FEM input manifest; passive quadrature/native-integral cross-check with raw-enough evidence; one instrumented F07 same-step and endpoint CSV with code diff, energy increments and verdict; changes to stale metadata and Doc68 interpretation; optional phase/time weighted stirring comparison if making quantitative flow claims. Include the command line, git revision/build identity and exact acceptance criteria. The current evidence is enough to move forward without restarting the original EM shape investigation.

## 6. Scope of this review

I inspected submitted Docs 62–68, current geometry, thermal audit and stirring driver source, FEM geometry/model/integrator, runners, JSON summaries, CSV time ranges and solver logs, and independently checked the quoted ratios. The archive omits the large FEM `.pos`/`.msh` and Doc56 original flow CSV; I did not rerun the GPU/FEM solvers or claim independent validation of those omitted raw fields. The stale metadata discrepancy and the unconditional `|| true` test assertion follow directly from supplied files.

## 2. P0 — reproducibility

- `export_reference_csv.py` already takes `--mesh-file` / `--run-manifest` / correct defaults; `run_doc67_passive_heating.sh` now writes `run_manifest.json` and passes R/z/lc/Js.

## 3. P1 — F07 same-state audit

Flag: `--f07-state-audit` → `output/french_f07_state_audit.csv`.  
Runner: `reference/stirring_stage3_r0/doc70_f07_state_audit/run_doc70_f07_2s.sh` (same 2 s path as Doc68 + audit).

### Stages

| stage | Mass≈ρV? | typical `rel_l2` |
|---|---|---|
| `post_density_regularization` | **no** | 6e−3 … 2.8e−2 |
| `csv_energy_block` (after `AdvectionStepSetup`) | **yes** | ~4.2e−8 |
| `post_acoustic_2nd_half` / `post_thermal_q_diff_bc` | **no** | ~9e−3 … 1.9e−2 |
| `endpoint_as_left_by_time_loop` (this run) | **yes** | ~4.2e−8 |
| `endpoint_after_forced_density_regularization` | unchanged here | ~4.2e−8 |

### Mechanism (localized)

1. `DensitySummationCK` updates ρ from neighbors; `VolumetricMeasure` is still stale → Mass≠ρV.
2. `AdvectionStepSetup` sets `Vol = Mass/ρ` → Mass≈ρV. Energy CSV and Doc68 sampled rows sit **after** this restore.
3. Acoustic continuity updates ρ without refreshing Vol → Mass≠ρV grows again (O(1%), same magnitude as Doc68’s endpoint 0.01787). Doc68 ended at `advection_steps=600` mid-acoustic cycle (`sum_rho_vol=112.299`). This Doc70 run exited after one more advection (`601`) so the endpoint happened to be consistent — **not** a physics change.
4. Absolute `H_mass − H_rho0V` ≈ 85–110 J is the ρ0V vs m weight convention under the same T field; production ledger still uses ρ0V. Do not treat that gap as a conservation residual.

### Acceptance vs Doc69 P1

- Per-operator state mismatch **explained**: intended WCSPH split (summation → V=m/ρ → acoustic ρ update), not particle-index corruption.
- Endpoint Mass−ρV is a **sampling-phase** effect; report Mass−ρV only after `AdvectionStepSetup` (or document acoustic-phase values separately).
- Energy residual gate unchanged (`energy_residual_H_rel≈0.001`); no production H switch.

## 4. P2 narrow — passive quadrature

Same Doc67 coil-only `az.pos`, σ_eval=16, 2πr_c weighting:

| Integrand for \|A\|² | P_passive [W] | vs centroid |
|---|---:|---:|
| Centroid of nodal A, then square (production) | 2.206223 | — |
| Exact ∫\|A\|² for linear FE A | 2.206706 | **+0.022%** |
| Mean of nodal \|A_i\|² | 2.208154 | +0.088% |

Centroid under-square is negligible vs the ~3% (P0−P_passive) gap. Far-field / source / domain remain the drivers. Native GetDP passive integral still optional later.

## 5. Code touch list

- `test_3d_ophelie_french_stirring_em.cpp`: `--f07-state-audit`, snapshots, endpoint snap **before** any diagnostic dens-reg (diagnostic dens-reg only after production summary).

1. Prefer conservation / F07 claims at `csv_energy_block` phase (post `AdvectionStepSetup`); if quoting endpoint, also record `advection_steps` and whether Vol was just restored.
2. Controlled buoyancy / stirred thermal at short time (Doc69 P3) — still no 1000 s.
3. Optional: common-support SPH↔FEM source-only Q; separate R vs z far-field sweeps.
4. Do not retune Js from source-only SPH vs conducting FEM.

# File: `71_FRENCH_PAPER_PARAMETER_OBSERVABLE_MATRIX_2026-09-29.md`

# Doc 71 — French-paper parameter / observable matrix (P3 start)

Date: 2026-09-29  
Parent: Doc 69 §4 P3, Doc 70 §6  
Machine-readable: [`reference/french_paper_observable_matrix_v0.json`](reference/french_paper_observable_matrix_v0.json)  
Contract freeze: [`reference/literature_contract_v0.json`](reference/literature_contract_v0.json)

**Purpose:** name what each paper asks for, what the code implements, and what is still `OPEN`/`MISSING` — before claiming reproduction or launching long runs.

Journal PDFs were **not** re-read this cycle; provenance follows in-tree Docs 09/41/ASSUMPTIONS and headers.

## 1. Decision

| Item | Status |
|---|---|
| Two-regime matrix (CEP natural / CES stirred) | **done** (this doc + JSON) |
| Claim paper reproduction from numerical stability | **forbidden** |
| First scientific target | **Natural convection quantitative credibility** (CEP / thesis; Doc77) |
| Stirred T distribution / uniformity (CES) | **secondary** — maintain baselines; Doc76 cooling-blade / Fig.6 not mainline GPU |
| Natural meridional cell | **active** — after Doc77 A/B/C; Ø500 mm glass frozen (thesis) |
| 1000 s / Js retune / production `--aind=on` | **still deferred** |

## 2. CEP 2008 — natural convection

Citation: Jacoutot et al., *Chem. Eng. Process.* **47** (2008) 449–455.  
Code: `test_3d_ophelie_french_natural_convection_frozen_q`.

| Parameter | Paper / intent | Code now | Tag |
|---|---|---|---|
| Crucible Ø | 650 mm | reduced / natural reload | PAPER → CODE map |
| f | ~300 kHz (intro) | **282 kHz** default | **OPEN** |
| P_absorbed (example) | **50 kW** | `--target-absorbed-power=50000` | PAPER |
| Generator | 400 kW | not a glass target | PAPER (supply) |
| σ, ρ, cp, k @ 1473 K | Table 1: 16 / 2750 / 1150 / 4 | CEP material family | PAPER (+ ρ **OPEN** vs 2800) |
| Melt H | Fig.1 unlabeled | **0.185 m** | **OPEN** |
| Stirrer | none | 0 rpm | PAPER |
| EM | OPHELIE integral | 3D edge-flux; `--aind=off` | CODE |
| Time | long / stationary | short smoke only | **MISSING** long window |

| Observable | Paper | Code metric | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Meridional cell | CEP figures | `U_merid_mean`, circ proxy | **DEFERRED** (Doc37 inconclusive) |
| T field | CEP maps | T_mean/std, T_rz | OPEN; no contour claim |
| Ra–Nu | if quoted | `Nu_sph` only | DEFERRED; paper Nu **OPEN** |

## 3. CES 2008 — mechanically stirred

Citation: Jacoutot et al., *Chem. Eng. Sci.* **63** (2008) 2391–2401.  
Code: `test_3d_ophelie_french_stirring_em`.

| Parameter | Paper / intent | Code now | Tag |
|---|---|---|---|
| P_absorbed (example) | **~60 kW** | target 60 kW, fixed-current | PAPER |
| f | 300 kHz | 300 kHz | PAPER |
| Stirrer | **10 rpm** | 10 rpm | PAPER |
| Melt R, H | cold crucible / bath | CAD **R=0.25**, **H=0.21** | CODE |
| Coil stack | — | **0.23 m** | **OPEN** vs H |
| σ(1473) | Table-1 16 vs thesis | **~24.75** (IV.10) | **OPEN** |
| ρ0 | Table-1 2750 | ~**2800** stirring | **OPEN** |
| Skull | cold crucible / crust | Level-1 δ=5 mm + free crust | CODE (not dynamic) |
| `--balance-heat-loss` | — | **forbidden** for paper compare | CONTRACT |
| Js | — | `I/A` (Doc50) | CODE |
| Time | ~1000 s class | 1–10 rev observation | **MISSING** long; staged later |

| Observable | Paper | Code metric | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| **T distribution / uniformity** | CES T figures | T_mean, T_std, T_out/ctr/bot/top, T_rz | **FIRST TARGET** |
| Velocity / mixing | CES U figures | windowed `U_rms`, `U_theta_rms` | DIAGNOSTIC (not endpoint) |
| Torque / Np | if present | signed `torque_z` → P_mech; `Np_sph` | DIAGNOSTIC; paper Np **OPEN** |
| Nu | if present | `Nu_sph` | DIAGNOSTIC; paper Nu **OPEN** |
| Q shape | CES Q maps | q_rz; SPH↔FEM support match | PARTIAL (~10% residual kept) |

## 4. How to measure (locked by Docs 41/70)

1. **Mass−ρV:** only after `AdvectionStepSetup` (`csv_energy_block`), or label acoustic-phase explicitly.
2. **Energy:** `R_E = ΔH − E_Q + E_BC` with production H=Σ ρ0 cp V ΔT; provisional 2% on short multi-refresh runs.
3. **Stirring flow:** time-weighted (and if practical phase-matched) windows; never mode-compare on a single endpoint sample.
4. **Natural buoyancy:** any new twin must use Doc41 freeze (no Q/diffusion/BC drift); hydrostatic T(z) alone is weak — radial T(r) preferred before mesh spend.

## 5. Explicit next (engineering)

1. **This cycle:** time/phase-weighted stirring windows on archived monitor CSVs (Doc56/64/65/obs_5rev) — closes Doc69 P3 stirring comparability without a new GPU campaign.
2. Natural: optional short freeze-T twin only if a **non-curl-free** T driver is available; else keep deferred.
3. After short-run T/flow evidence: staged longer observation (e.g. 5-rev T_std drift), still **no 1000 s**.

## 6. Non-claims

- Matrix ≠ reproduction.
- Power book at 50/60 kW ≠ correct Q map.
- `validation_passed=1` on 2 s ≠ paper temperature agreement.

<a id="72-stir-time-phase-weighted-windows-2026-09-29md"></a>

# File: `72_STIR_TIME_PHASE_WEIGHTED_WINDOWS_2026-09-29.md`

# Doc 72 — Time/phase-weighted stirring windows (P3 stirring comparability)

Date: 2026-09-29  
Parent: Doc 71 §5, Doc 69 §4 P3  
Tool: `reference/stirring_stage3_r0/analyze_stir_time_phase_windows.py`  
Output: `reference/stirring_stage3_r0/doc71_stir_time_phase_windows.json`

**No new GPU run.** Reprocessed archived `french_stirring_em_monitor.csv` (Doc56/64/65, obs_5rev; Doc70 listed for completeness, t≤2 s).

## 1. Decision

| Item | Status |
|---|---|
| Time-weighted `U_rms` / `U_theta` / signed `torque_z` over t∈[1,5] s | **done** |
| Endpoint-only flow compare for binary/mode identity | **rejected** (again) |
| Rotor phase-8 bins | **diagnostic** (coarse; empty bins at sparse cadence) |
| Paper Np/Nu match | **still OPEN** (`_sph` only) |

## 2. Metric definition

For irregular CSV samples \((t_i, v_i)\):

\[
\langle v\rangle_t = \frac{\sum_i \tfrac12(v_i+v_{i+1})\,\Delta t_i}{\sum_i \Delta t_i}
\]

Phase: fractional revolution \(\{\mathrm{rev}\}\) into 8 bins (counts can be empty at `--csv-every=100`).

## 3. Results (t∈[1,5] s where available)

| Case | n | \(\langle U_\mathrm{rms}\rangle_t\) | \(\langle U_\theta\rangle_t\) | \(\langle T_z\rangle_t\) [N·m] | endpoint \(U_\mathrm{rms}\) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Doc56 1-rev | 58 | **0.10615** | 0.06520 | −0.379 | 0.1125 |
| Doc64 1-rev | 58 | **0.10589** | 0.06516 | −0.370 | 0.0993 |
| Doc65 cadence100 | 11 | 0.10093 | 0.06243 | −0.350 | 0.1146 |
| obs_5rev | 58 | **0.10532** | 0.06481 | −0.387 | 0.0812 |

Relative to Doc56 on the same window:

| Compare | \(\Delta\langle U_\mathrm{rms}\rangle_t\) | \(\Delta\) endpoint \(U_\mathrm{rms}\) |
|---|---:|---:|
| Doc64 / Doc56 | **−0.24%** | −11.7% |
| obs_5rev / Doc56 | **−0.78%** | −27.8% |
| Doc65 / Doc56 | −4.9% (sparse samples) | +1.8% |

Doc70 (2 s only): window truncated; do not use for 1–5 s identity.

## 4. Reading

1. After Doc62/63 frame repair, Doc56↔Doc64 **time-weighted** stirring velocity agrees to ≪1%; the large endpoint swings are phase/noise, not an EM-frame regression.
2. Signed torque time-mean (~−0.37…−0.39 N·m) is stable enough for `P_mech` / `Np_sph` trends; still not paper Np.
3. Phase-8 means vary ~0.10–0.11 m/s within a revolution — endpoint can land anywhere in that band.
4. Sparse cadence (Doc65, every 100) biases window means; prefer csv-every≤20 for quantitative flow claims.

## 5. Explicit next

1. First paper-facing observable: **stirred T uniformity** on a short multi-refresh constant-property run (matrix §3), reporting T_std / T_out/ctr with the same window discipline — no 1000 s.
2. Natural cell remains deferred until a non-hydrostatic T driver exists.
3. Optional: denser phase bins on a dedicated 1-rev archive with csv-every=5–10.

# File: `73_STIRRED_T_UNIFORMITY_WINDOW_BASELINE_2026-09-29.md`

# Doc 73 — Stirred T-uniformity window baseline (P3 first scientific target)

Date: 2026-09-29  
Parent: Doc 71 §3 (CES first target), Doc 72 §5  
Artifacts:
- `reference/stirring_stage3_r0/doc73_t_uniformity_1rev/`
- `reference/stirring_stage3_r0/analyze_stir_t_uniformity_windows.py`
- `reference/stirring_stage3_r0/doc73_t_uniformity_windows.json`

## 1. Decision

| Item | Status |
|---|---|
| Fresh 1-rev baseline on post-Doc70 binary (`validation_passed=1`) | **done** |
| Time-weighted T uniformity protocol (`T_std`, `T_out−T_ctr`, `T_bot−T_top`) | **done** |
| Match Doc56/64 on t∈[1,5] s T windows | **yes** (≪1% on T_std_tw) |
| Stationary uniformity / paper T contours | **not claimed** — T_std still grows (obs_5rev) |
| 1000 s / Js retune / `--aind=on` | **still deferred** |

## 2. Run identity (Doc73 1 rev)

Same contract as Doc64 / Stage-3 r0:

```text
--thermo-em-coupling=periodic --em-control=fixed-current --aind=off
--skull-resistance --skull-delta=0.005 --free-crust-resistance --free-crust-delta=0.005
--constant-thermal-rheology --require-validation --boundary-area-normalize
--end-time=6.0 --em-update-interval=0.5 --csv-every=20 --screen-every=20
```

| Gate / book | Value |
|---|---:|
| `validation_passed` | **1** |
| `energy_residual_H_rel` | **0.00088** |
| periodic refreshes | 11 |
| `P_joule` | 59.85 kW |
| wall clock | ≈0.036 h |

## 3. Uniformity metrics (time-weighted)

Definition: trapezoidal ⟨·⟩_t on monitor CSV. Prefer **t∈[1,5] s** for 1-rev identity; use **rev∈[4,5]** for longer obs.

### 3.1 One-revolution identity (t∈[1,5] s)

| Case | ⟨T_std⟩_t [K] | ⟨T_mean⟩_t [K] | ⟨T_out−T_ctr⟩_t [K] | ⟨T_bot−T_top⟩_t [K] |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Doc56 | 2.147 | 1472.802 | −0.170 | 0.728 |
| Doc64 | 2.149 | 1472.802 | −0.168 | 0.723 |
| Doc73 (this) | **2.145** | **1472.803** | **−0.167** | **0.727** |
| obs_5rev (same early window) | 2.184 | 1472.781 | −0.092 | 0.634 |

Doc73 vs Doc64 on ⟨T_std⟩_t: **−0.15%**. Binary/stack after Doc70 F07 instrumentation does **not** change the 1-rev thermal uniformity baseline.

Endpoint `T_std` (~4.2 K) is larger than the window mean because T_std rises through the first revolution — same rule as flow: **do not mode-compare on endpoint alone**.

### 3.2 Evolution (obs_5rev archive)

| Window | ⟨T_std⟩_t [K] | ⟨T_bot−T_top⟩_t [K] |
|---|---:|---:|
| rev 0–1 | 2.19 | 0.65 |
| rev 4–5 | **15.12** | **5.44** |
| Growth (rev0–1 → rev4–5) | **+12.93 K** | — |

T field is **not** approaching a short-time plateau. Any CES-style uniformity claim needs a stated time/rev window and an uncertainty band for ongoing drift — not a single final sample.

## 4. What this closes / leaves open

**Closes for planning:** operational T-uniformity observables + window protocol; post-Doc70 1-rev regression green; Doc56/64/73 mutual consistency on early windows.

**Still OPEN:** paper figure digitization; σ-table / ρ0 OPEN_PARAMETERs; Q-shape ~10%; long-time (≫5 rev) approach to paper stationarity; natural cell.

## 5. Explicit next

1. Optional sensitivity on the **same 1-rev window**: e.g. `--em-update-interval=1` or normalize-off — report Δ⟨T_std⟩_t only (already partially archived under `sens_*`).
2. If extending observation: another 5-rev with current binary only after deciding the acceptance band for T_std drift (engineering, not paper).
3. Still no 1000 s; still no Js retune.

# File: `74_T_UNIFORMITY_SENSITIVITY_AND_DRIFT_BAND_2026-09-29.md`

# Doc 74 — T-uniformity sensitivity on matched windows + drift band

Date: 2026-09-29  
Parent: Doc 73 §5  
JSON: [`reference/stirring_stage3_r0/doc74_t_uniformity_sensitivity.json`](reference/stirring_stage3_r0/doc74_t_uniformity_sensitivity.json)  
Archives: Stage-3 `sens_*` (Doc 44) reprocessed with Doc 73 time-weighted protocol vs Doc73/Doc64 baselines. **No new GPU run.**

## 1. Decision

| Item | Status |
|---|---|
| Re-report Doc44 sensitivities with ⟨·⟩_t on t∈[1,5] s | **done** |
| EM interval 0.5→1.0 s on ⟨T_std⟩_t | **robust** (~+1.4%) |
| Area-normalize off | **material BC-measure effect** (~−8% T_std_tw, ~−8% loss) — keep ON for literature path |
| Skull/crust δ 5→10 mm | **model-parameter lever** (~−34% T_std_tw / loss) — not a numerical gate |
| Engineering 1-rev noise band for ⟨T_std⟩_t | **proposed** (§4) |
| Paper contours / 1000 s | **still OPEN / deferred** |

## 2. Window protocol (locked)

- Primary: trapezoidal mean on `french_stirring_em_monitor.csv`, **t∈[1,5] s** (same as Doc72/73).
- Baseline for Δ: **Doc73** 1-rev (`⟨T_std⟩_t = 2.145 K`). Doc64 differs by −0.15% (Doc73).
- Historical `sens_*` are Stage-3 binaries; early-window T agrees with Doc73, so relative Δ remain usable.

## 3. Results vs Doc73 (t∈[1,5] s)

| Case | ⟨T_std⟩_t [K] | Δ rel | ⟨T_mean⟩_t | Δ loss | notes |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
| Doc73 baseline | **2.145** | — | 1472.803 | — | validate=1, E_rel=0.00088 |
| `sens_em_interval_1s` | 2.175 | **+1.4%** | 1472.781 | +0.2% | 5 refreshes vs 11 |
| `sens_no_area_normalize` | 1.974 | **−8.0%** | 1472.939 | **−7.6%** | raw SPH shell A |
| `sens_skull_delta_10mm` | 1.420 | **−33.8%** | 1473.348 | **−33.9%** | δ=10 mm; h_side drops |

Also: `dT_out−ctr` and `dT_bot−top` move more under normalize-off / skull-δ than under EM interval (see JSON). Endpoint-only Doc44 tables told the same qualitative story; window means remove endpoint U_rms noise from the comparison.

## 4. Engineering acceptance band (1-rev claims)

From Doc73 mutual consistency and the EM-interval probe:

| Class | Suggested rule |
|---|---|
| **Numerical / schedule noise** (same physics flags) | matched-window \|Δ⟨T_std⟩_t\| ≲ **5%** or ≲ **0.15 K** |
| **BC measure / area normalize** | report explicitly; production literature path keeps normalize **ON** |
| **Skull/crust δ** | **model OPEN_PARAMETER**; do not treat 34% loss swing as mesh/dt failure (Doc45) |
| **Time drift** (obs_5rev) | ⟨T_std⟩_t grows ~**3.2 K/rev** over rev0–1→4–5; **not** converged uniformity |

Do **not** claim CES stationary uniformity inside the first few revolutions.

## 5. What this closes

- Doc73 “optional sensitivity on same window”: answered without a new campaign.
- Separates **numerical robustness** (EM interval) from **model levers** (normalize, skull δ).
- Gives a concrete band for future 1-rev binary/regression compares of T_std.

## 6. Explicit next

1. Keep Doc73 1-rev + this band as the stirred T regression reference.
2. Before any longer obs with current binary: state whether the goal is drift characterization or a quasi-steady claim (latter needs much longer time and is still deferred).
3. Optional later: digitize CES T figure targets into the matrix (still OPEN provenance).
4. Still no 1000 s / Js retune / production `--aind=on`.

## 6. Digitization checklist (when PDF available)

# GPT discussion brief — post Doc 69–75 (French reproduction handoff)

Date: 2026-09-29  
Audience: external GPT review  
Pack: `docs/ophelie/packs/gpt_doc69_to_75_discussion_2026-09-29.tar.gz`

## A. Where we are (accepted locally after Doc69)

| Thread | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Doc67 FEM metadata stale (R=1.0, Js=2πI/A) | **Corrected** + `run_manifest.json` | Doc70 / regenerated `metadata.json` |

| Passive \|A\|² centroid quadrature | **~0.02%** vs exact linear integrand — not the ~3% P0 gap | Doc70 quadrature JSON |

**Still deferred by policy:** 1000 s, production `--aind=on`, Js retune, paper-contour T claim, natural cell mesh refine.

## B. Questions for GPT (please answer explicitly)

1. **F07 closure:** Accept that endpoint `rel_l2(Mass,ρV)≈0.018` is an intended WCSPH phase (post-acoustic / pre-`AdvectionStepSetup`), and that literature/energy claims should use `csv_energy_block` / post-`AdvectionStepSetup` state? Or do you still want a production change (e.g. always report Mass−ρV after setup)?

2. **FEM power split:** Confirm we must **not** call “half truncation / half feedback” universal, and must **not** retune Js from source-only SPH vs conducting FEM. Is the next EM item (a) farther domain with separate R vs z, (b) common-support SPH↔FEM Q, or (c) deprioritize EM until CES T digitization?

3. **P3 scientific path:** With Doc73–74 T baseline + Doc75 empty paper columns, is the correct stop/go **stop for CES PDF digitization** before more GPU time? Or is a controlled multi-rev **drift characterization** (not quasi-steady claim) still useful without paper numbers?

4. **Noise band:** Is the proposed 1-rev band \|Δ⟨T_std⟩_t\| ≲ 5% or ≲0.15 K acceptable as engineering regression tolerance under identical physics flags?

5. **Natural branch:** Keep deferred until a non-hydrostatic T(r) driver exists, or reopen a short Doc41-freeze twin with hot-bottom only for documentation?

6. **Stop / go this turn:** Prefer (A) wait for digitized Fig.6 probes, (B) one more EM common-support Q map, or (C) 5-rev T_std drift re-baseline on current binary — pick one primary.

## C. Numbers cheat-sheet

**FEM (I=1 A, σ_eval=16):**  
P0=2.275 W; P_passive(R=1.5)=2.206; P_conducting=2.129; mode gap≈0.077 W; domain R=2.0 → P_passive=2.240; centroid vs exact linear \|A\|² ≈ **+0.022%**.

**F07 (Doc70, 2 s):**  
After `AdvectionStepSetup` / CSV: `rel_l2≈4e−8`. After acoustic: `rel_l2~0.01–0.019`. Endpoint this run after adv=601: consistent (~4e−8). Doc68 endpoint mismatch = mid-cycle sample.

**Flow (t∈[1,5] s):**  
⟨U_rms⟩_t Doc56/64 ≈ 0.10615 / 0.10589 (−0.24%).

**T (Doc73, 1 rev):**  
⟨T_std⟩_t≈2.145 K; endpoint T_std≈4.21 K; E_rel≈0.088%; validation_passed=1.  
obs_5rev: ⟨T_std⟩_t grows ~2.2→15.1 K (rev0–1→4–5).

## D. Please do / don’t

**Do:** challenge F07 “phase not bug”, EM priorities vs digitization blocker, and the 5%/0.15 K band.  
**Don’t:** invent CES figure temperatures; don’t propose 1000 s / Picard-on / Js retune / skull tune to force T agreement unless a new physical question requires them.

<a id="76-ces-stirring-implementation-plan-2026-09-30md"></a>

# File: `76_CES_STIRRING_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_2026-09-30.md`

# Doc76：按 CES 搅拌论文推进的实现与验证任务

日期：2026-09-30。交给 Cursor 执行。

## 0. 优先级修订（2026-09-30）

甲方最新需求：自然对流算例优先达到可信定量对照；搅拌结果允许一定偏差。因此本文件的搅拌P0–P3任务降为次要工作，不再作为自然对流开发的前置门槛。主线执行Doc77的基础验证→自然圆柱诊断→文献对照。

用户已确认作者博士论文给出的玻璃域直径为500 mm。固定当前玻璃域直径，不再以650 mm坩埚直径或未经确认的340 mm替换。归档博士论文页码/截图，并注明500 mm对应的工况；自然对流液位、桨与线圈等其余尺寸仍分别核对。

保留本文件冷却桨、边界物性及初态任务，作为搅拌模型差异记录和后续改进；不要求完成Fig.6精确匹配后才推进自然对流。搅拌工程结果可保留，但必须声明常物性/冷却桨/初态等实际简化。

## 1. 用户决定与目标

用户已确认 **500 mm 玻璃域直径**，保留现有 CAD，不缩放成 650 mm 或 340 mm。其余物理设置以以下论文为依据：

Jacoutot et al., *Numerical modeling of coupled phenomena in a mechanically stirred molten-glass bath heated by induction*, Chemical Engineering Science 63 (2008), 2391–2401, DOI: 10.1016/j.ces.2008.01.026。

目标分两步：先建立按文献设置、能量可审计的搅拌算例，再进行论文温度与流场对照。Doc73 的常物性、一转结果保留为工程回归基线，不直接替换或称为论文验证。

本文依据用户上传论文及 gpt_geometry_bc_fig6_audit_2026-09-29.tar.gz。以下已确认差异应直接推进；未明确给出的文献信息必须记录来源或标 OPEN，不能拟造。

## 2. 首先修正论文图号与参数记录（P0）

更新 Doc71、Doc75 及对应 JSON：

| 图 | 内容与对照条件 |
|---|---|
| Fig.2 | 黏度、热容、导热率、电导率的温度依赖曲线 |
| Fig.5 | 无桨轴对称耦合计算得到的初始热源；Qmax=1.28e7 W/m³ |
| Fig.6 | A/B 点温度随时间曲线，比较每 1/5/10 个热流体时间步进行一次电磁耦合；横轴 0–150 s |
| Fig.7 | 1000 s 凝壳形状，局部侧面约 1 cm、底部约 1.5 cm；不是全域均匀厚度 |
| Fig.8 | 1000 s 无量纲温度场；Tmax=1620 K；标有 A/B 点 |
| Fig.9 | 1000 s 流场；Vmax=0.22 m/s |
| Fig.10 | 1000 s 耦合热源；Qmax=6.64e6 W/m³ |

正文第4.1节说明结果按变量最大数值归一化。实现对照前核实温度归一化使用 K 还是转换后的温标，保存定义；不要将轴对称平均温度与局部三维截面直接等同。

论文 Fig.6 的 A/B 初温粗读约 1334/1275 K，仅用于识别初态不同，不作为精确数值或强制初始点温度。当前统一1473 K不能直接拿来验证这条时间曲线。

## 3. 热边界改为 CES 文献设置（P1，优先实现）

创建显式的 CES 参数配置或模式；日志保存所有实际生效参数及其来源，避免沿用自然对流默认值。

| 参数 | CES 设置 | 当前基线 |
|---|---:|---:|
| 非旋转冷壁换热系数 | 300 W/(m² K) | 侧300、底35；另串联凝壳 |
| 冷却桨壁换热系数 | 2500 W/(m² K) | 未见热更新路径 |
| 冷却温度 Tc | 333 K | 300 K |
| 自由表面对流 hs | 20 W/(m² K) | 20 |
| 对流环境 Ta | 303 K | 300 K |
| 辐射环境 Tar | 423 K | 300 K |
| 表面发射率 | 0.9 | 0.8 |

来源：论文 Table1、式(5)(6)、第4.1节。非旋转冷壁300包括底部的应用应在配置中明确记录；当前35不能继续标为本篇CES值。

### 3.1 冷却桨必须接入热更新和账本

上传源码中，转子参与机械接触、压力/黏性力和扭矩；热扩散只创建玻璃 Inner 粒子对，外边界算子按圆柱侧/底/自由面施加，未见转子冷却。

实现转子表面的独立热通量 q=h(Tglass−Tc)。可以采用与移动转子接触关系一致的 Robin 边界离散，不要求先实现金属内部温度求解。不要将每个接触粒子都乘以桨叶总面积。

要求：
- 分清浸没的桨表面与自由表面以上部分；更新位置/接触后边界权重正确跟随旋转。
- 壁、桨、底、自由表面标签避免重复施加同一表面通量；角部多面通量按各面面积计量。
- 分别输出面积和散热功率：side、bottom、rotor、free convection、free radiation，以及任何固定凝壳/结壳影响。
- E_rotor_out从实际热更新每一步累计，纳入总E_boundary_out和验证门槛，独立于日志频率。
- 允许关闭rotor冷却以作受控诊断；日志必须显式表明开关状态。

短验证：均匀温度下功率与 h A_wet (T−Tc) 的离散一致性；关闭桨冷却后该项为零；旋转不会凭空改变浸没面积；累计散热与施加的温度变化能量一致。再做1转CES参数运行，不能沿用旧总passed作为实现证明。

### 3.2 凝壳与自由表面结壳

论文通过高黏度区表示固化玻璃，μ>1e4 Pa s作为固化判定，厚度随位置变化；当前固定5 mm串联热阻是简化模型。

- 先明确500 mm表示全部玻璃域还是已扣除凝壳的流动域，由用户确认尺寸后补齐。
- 如果域内已包含并解析高黏度凝壳，不再无依据串联同一层固定热阻。
- 如果采用固定热阻替代未解析的凝壳，可保留为简化分支，但不能声称复现Fig.7；厚度与导热率必须有来源或OPEN标记。
- 当前额外free-crust模型不是论文自由表面式(5)的默认组成。CES对照模式默认用论文对流+辐射；额外结壳单列为模型扩展，不为匹配温度自动添加。
- 不调凝壳厚度或整体热损失缩放去强行匹配Fig.6。

## 4. 核对并启用对应玻璃的温度物性（P2）

密度2800 kg/m³，热膨胀系数3e-5 K⁻¹，层流牛顿流体、Boussinesq浮力、不计Lorentz力及内部辐射：按CES实现并记录。

浮力式参考温度293 K与热初始温度、焓参考温度是不同量。不能把293 K当成初始熔体温度；如改浮力参考值，核对静水压力/体力基准，而不是机械替换一个T0。

逐项核对μ(T)、cp(T)、k(T)、σ(T)与Fig.2。现有博士论文IV.10拟合可以作为候选，必须确认玻璃成分及曲线一致；不要混入另一篇CEP的Table1常数而标为CES物性。正文未给完整拟合系数时，数字化Fig.2并保存温度、值、误差和拟合范围。

检查σ上限30 S/m、黏度上下限和温度截断是否扭曲目标温度区间。若为数值稳定而截断，显式输出截断粒子比例及能量影响。变cp使用一致的焓定义；变μ路径说明目前是局部黏度还是全域平均黏度，平均黏度不能自动称为论文局部μ(T)实现。

## 5. 初态、A/B监测与运行协议（P3）

先检查能否在同一目标玻璃域生成无桨轴对称热—电磁耦合初态。现有natural frozen-Q使用不同几何及冻结热源，不能直接当作论文预热初态。

实现温度初态保存/加载以及状态转移检查。将预热温度映射到带桨玻璃粒子时，明确被桨占据区域、插值误差和初始热能变化；速度/压力初始化与热初态分别说明。若无法得到论文完全相同初态，声明“稳态空间比较”为主要目标，Fig.6瞬态曲线只作定性对照。

A/B在Fig.8靠近桨叶，是局部点，不等于T_center/T_outer或轴对称分箱平均。论文没有明确数字坐标时：从图确定相对位置并记录不确定性；确认取点在旋转框架还是实验室框架。桨附近局部比较优先使用跟随桨的采样定义，输出实际坐标、采样半径、粒子数和温度，不能将最近非空分箱替代精确点而不标注。

论文热流体步长0.2 s，1/5/10步耦合对应0.2/1/2 s。SPH声学步不等于此步长，应按物理时间设置电磁刷新并记录实际刷新时刻。

## 6. 电磁模型与功率控制的必要检查

保留当前已校准源场基线。核对线圈半径285 mm、7匝、堆叠230 mm、电流方向及底板/分段坩埚来源。500 mm由用户确认，不阻塞热边界实现，但最终几何和线圈应作为一套配置一致冻结。

区分：温度→σ→Q更新与感应电流反馈场，两者不是同一个开关。aind=off是简化近似；在对应σ/T范围做少量同条件场和Q分布对照，决定其误差是否可接受。不要只用总功率一致证明热源位置正确。

论文各图注明Ptot=60 kW，但本文未明确展示整个瞬态的电源控制律。建立固定电流与固定吸收功率的显式模式，核实文献控制含义；不擅自把“始终60 kW”认定为已证实的论文控制策略。每次刷新保存电流、吸收功率及变化。

## 7. 分阶段运行与验收

执行顺序：P0文档修正 → P1边界与桨冷却短测试 → P2物性路径核对 → P3初态和探针 → 分阶段长时观察。

先6 s短运行，再30/60/150 s等阶段；根据温度、热源、散热、转矩演化决定是否延长至1000 s。每阶段能续算且保存配置、commit、dirty diff、二进制/初态hash和计算成本。

验收分开报告：
1. 工程：无崩溃、实际刷新计划完整、有效传热、边界能量账本完整。
2. 数值：同一算子阶段比较质量/体积/焓，报告相对总通量及净储能的能量残差；关键温度/桨散热指标的时间步和空间敏感性。
3. 物理：参数来源明确、初态与控制模式声明、凝壳近似说明。
4. 文献：A/B曲线或Fig.8–10空间分布，在可比工况和时间状态下比较。

5%或0.15 K只能作为暂定工程报警阈值，不代表论文精度或收敛通过。不要再仅靠一转passed或总功率接近宣称复现。

## 8. 本轮给用户的交付

- 更新的论文参数/图号JSON与Markdown；所有缺失项逐项标记。
- 代码修改清单和配置文件；保留Doc73原始基线。
- 桨叶与各边界的面积、瞬时功率、累计能量CSV。
- 一次短CES配置测试的命令、原始日志、能量账本和温度/热源输出。
- 无桨热初态和A/B采样的实现方案，注明是否已实现。
- 尚需用户确认的几何只列具体尺寸问题；其余已明确论文参数自主推进。

<!-- ===== BEGIN 77_NATURAL_CONVECTION_BENCHMARK_AND_PRIORITY_PLAN_2026-09-30.md ===== -->

<a id="77-natural-convection-benchmark-and-priority-plan-2026-09-30md"></a>

# File: `77_NATURAL_CONVECTION_BENCHMARK_AND_PRIORITY_PLAN_2026-09-30.md`

# Doc77：自然对流主线与浮力标准验证任务

日期：2026-09-30。给Cursor执行；与修订后的Doc76配套。

## 1. 目标与已确认事项

甲方优先要求法国自然对流算例定量可信，搅拌算例允许一定偏差。自然对流成为主线；不要等待搅拌冷却桨或Fig.6完全匹配。

用户已确认作者博士论文中的玻璃域直径500 mm。固定直径，归档原文页码/截图；确认其对应自然/搅拌工况及液位高度。650 mm坩埚直径不直接等于玻璃域直径。

现有自然分支的结论是“定量环流未完成，旧受控测试结论不足”，不是已证明SPH无法求解。不要沿用旧文档“natural no-go”的措辞。Doc41中自然分支延期和禁止加密的旧排程，被本次用户的自然优先需求替代；加密由以下验证结果决定。

## 2. 采用同一生产浮力路径做三个基础测试

建立小型独立case，尽量复用自然生产的Boussinesq力、压力/黏性、热更新及边界算子。明确测试和生产共享哪些路径，避免只验证一个新写的独立公式。先查仓库是否已有适用case，再扩展。

### A. 稳定竖直分层静水平衡：有解析解

封闭矩形域，z向上，重力g向下；给定并冻结T(z)=Tref+a z，a>0（顶部热、底部冷，稳定分层）。关闭Q、扩散和热边界更新，排除温度漂移；四周不动。不要用自由表面启动晃动做第一项验证。

Boussinesq体力为−ρ0 g ez + ρ0 g β(T−Tref) ez。总压力解析式：

p(z)=p(0)−ρ0 g z+ρ0 g β a z²/2。

如代码求解减去静水压力后的修正压力，解析修正量为ρ0 g β a z²/2；压力比较先减去任意常数。若代码浮力参考温度不同，使用对应积分式重新生成参考，不能机械比较上述式。

WCSPH实际以状态方程求压时，按其EOS建立相容的密度/压力初态；不要同时任意指定不相容的压力和密度。Boussinesq参考密度与WCSPH数值密度分别说明。

输出：压力剖面误差、浮力解析值与实际施加力误差、速度rms/max及其随时间变化。做两档以上粒子间距，检验残余流动是否下降。均匀T=Tref的重力静水测试作为配套零浮力检查。

通过条件：力的符号/量纲正确，压力与体力一致，无持续增大的非物理环流，残余速度和压力误差随精化改善。阈值由测试尺度预先定义，不能仅Umax<c0即通过。

注意：竖直T(z)的浮力可被压力梯度平衡；热底冷顶可能失稳，取决于Ra、扰动和时间。因此“不产生环流”本身不是浮力失效证据。

### B. 一维导热：验证温度更新和边界

关闭流动和浮力，常ρ/cp/k，α=k/(ρcp)，0≤x≤L；固定两端Th/Tc。

稳态解析Tss=Th+(Tc−Th)x/L，热通量q=k(Th−Tc)/L。

建议用非平凡瞬态解直接验证扩散速度：
T(x,t)=Tss(x)+A sin(πx/L) exp(−απ²t/L²)。

两端Dirichlet，初态取t=0解析值；横向绝热或周期以实现一维问题。采用生产温度小增量/焓更新路径，另检查1473 K绝对温度背景下小增量能否累计。

输出温度L2/Linf误差、正弦模态衰减率、热通量和能量账。分别做时间步与粒子间距检查；避免只证明扰动会衰减而未证明衰减速率正确。

### C. de Vahl Davis二维方腔：完整自然对流数值基准

参考：G. de Vahl Davis (1983), Natural convection of air in a square cavity: A bench mark numerical solution, IJNMF 3,249–264, DOI:10.1002/fld.1650030305。

原文入口：https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.1002/fld.1650030305
公开原文副本：https://elearning.univ-bejaia.dz/pluginfile.php/310888/mod_resource/content/0/Ref%20%5B4%5D.pdf

这是高精度数值参考，不是闭式理论解。先获取原文表格并保存原始参考值；未核实的数据不能只凭记忆写入验收JSON。

方腔边长L，左热右冷，上下绝热，四周无滑移，封闭顶壁，无自由表面；常物性、Pr=0.71、Boussinesq；先Ra=1e3，再1e4。通过后按需要加1e5，不立即投入1e6。

定义ν=μ/ρ0，α=k/(ρ0 cp)，Ra=gβΔT L³/(να)，Pr=ν/α；保持βΔT≪1，避免用浮力的数值密度变化替代Boussinesq物性。

θ=(T−Tc)/ΔT，x*=x/L，t*=tα/L²，u*=uL/α。热壁Nu(y*)=−∂θ/∂x*，平均Nu为壁高积分。必须与原文速度无量纲、坐标和取样剖面一致。

输出：平均热/冷壁Nu及两侧平衡、中心线u/v剖面、极值及位置、温度/环流图、准稳态判据。左侧热流应上升、右侧冷流下降。不要只比较Umax或总热量。

二维实现优先；若只具备3D路径，采用出平面周期且证明近二维，不能把有前后无滑移壁的薄腔直接叫二维基准。

验收建议（工程目标，非原文标准）：先取得正确环流；精化后Nu相对参考误差目标≤3%，中心线速度极值目标≤5%，并检查极值位置。至少两档分辨率；趋势异常时加第三档。误差目标不能代替时间稳态与空间敏感性证据。WCSPH另报告Ma=Umax/c0、密度扰动和声速敏感性。

## 3. 通过基础测试后返回法国自然圆柱

采用现有自然case，同一粒子初态、几何、温度场和时间步，做开/关浮力对照；先冻结Q和T，用平滑径向温差T(r,z)建立非纯静水驱动。保存温度与坐标定义，不用总速度最大值代表环流。

比较时间平均ur/uz剖面、子午环流图、浮力开关差异和启动背景流。均匀温度/关浮力运行用于衡量自由表面与静水初始化噪声。不得将竖直剖面旧测试“未见环流”继续当作失败结论。

若A失败：先修压力/浮力符号、初态与平衡。
若B失败：先修热扩散/边界/状态精度。
若C失败而A/B通过：查压力-黏性耦合、边界、稳态时间、分辨率及WCSPH声速；不要先调法国热源。
若C通过但法国冻结径向温差响应被淹没：重点查粒子初态、自由表面噪声、玻璃高黏度与时间尺度。
若受控圆柱通过但文献不符：转查自然论文物性、热源分布、冷壁/凝壳、液位、时间状态及电磁反馈。

## 4. 法国文献工况核对

自然工况以对应CEP论文和博士论文自然对流章节为准，不直接使用CES的60 kW、冷却桨或搅拌Table1。当前自然记录常用50 kW、R=0.25 m、H=0.185 m、dp≈15 mm；这些分别核对来源，特别是高度和频率。

核对μ(T)、cp(T)、k(T)、σ(T)、β、热源空间分布、壁面/自由面边界、凝壳、初态和论文测量时刻。先固定可靠Q做热流体测试，再恢复温度→σ→EM更新。总功率标定不能证明空间Q正确。

分阶段延长运行，按温度、环流、散热和储能是否稳定决定时长；不以1000 s作为所有工况的统一稳态标准。基础方腔Pr=0.71验证后，需额外覆盖玻璃高Pr/高黏度范围，不能把空气基准通过等同玻璃工况全部通过。

## 5. 本轮Cursor应交付

1. 优先级变更和500 mm来源条目；修订相关旧文档。
2. A/B/C case源码、可运行命令和生产复用路径说明。
3. A与B解析参考生成、误差CSV及分辨率/步长比较。
4. C原始文献参考表、无量纲定义、实际参数及剖面/Nu对照。
5. 逐项结论：通过、失败、尚未运行；明确失败对应的下一步，不用一个总passed覆盖所有物理。
6. 自然圆柱最近开/关浮力命令、日志和ur/uz平均CSV；在拿到自然论文之前不猜文献参数。

先完成A/B和低Ra方腔，再决定自然圆柱加密。搅拌仅维护已有运行基线，Doc76冷却桨和Fig.6任务暂不占用主线计算资源。

Date: 2026-10-01  
Parent: [Doc77](77_NATURAL_CONVECTION_BENCHMARK_AND_PRIORITY_PLAN_2026-09-30.md)

> **Doc84 disposition (2026-10-03):** 旧 `passed=1` / “qualified accept” 仅表示 **force_audit + smoke（无 runaway）**。  
> **静水平衡未闭环**（残余 U 大、EOS p(z) OPEN）。勿将本节解读为自然对流定量验收完成。见 [Doc84](84_NATURAL_CONVECTION_REVIEW_AND_NEXT_ACTIONS_2026-10-03.md)。

## 0. Priority freeze (done)

| Item | Status |
|---|---|
| Glass Ø **500 mm** (thesis; user-confirmed) | frozen in Doc76/77 + README |
| Natural = client mainline | Doc71 first target updated |
| Retract blanket “natural no-go” | Doc19 supersession + Doc41 R10 note |

## 1. Doc77-A — stratified hydrostatic (closed box)

**Case:** `test_3d_ophelie_boussinesq_stratified_hydrostatic`  
**Reuse path:** `GravityForceCK` + production `BoussinesqBuoyancyForceCK` + Acoustic Riemann + `ViscousForceWithWall` + `DensitySummation` / `DensityRegularization<Internal>`  
**Logs:** [`reference/doc77a_runs/`](reference/doc77a_runs/)

| Run | force_ok | residual_ok | no_growth | no_runaway | passed |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| stratified dp=0.025 | 1 | 1 | 1 | 1 | **1** |
| uniform dp=0.025 | 1 | 1 | 1 | 1 | **1** |
| stratified dp=0.02 | 1 | 1 | 1 | 1 | **1** |

### Per-criterion (honest)

| Criterion | Result |
|---|---|
| Buoyancy force sign / magnitude vs −m β (T−Tref) g | **PASS** (`rel_err=0`, Fz>0 for hot-top) |
| Residual U after settle does not grow | **PASS** |
| No runaway (U_max ≪ c0) | **PASS** |
| EOS pressure vs analytic hydrostatic p(z) | **OPEN / diagnostic only** under `DensitySummation` (ρ rewritten →ρ0; quadratic fit fails). Continuity-only path blew up (U~c0). |
| Residual decreases with refine | **NOT shown** (coarse U_rms≈0.19, fine≈0.24) |

**Verdict A (Doc84):** `force_audit_pass` 成立；`hydrostatic_pass` **未通过**（残余流未压到弱浮力用途门槛；细化未降 U_rms）。旧 “qualified accept” 仅指算子接通 + smoke。

### Commands

```bash
BIN=build/tests/extra_source_and_tests/3d_examples/test_3d_ophelie_boussinesq_stratified_hydrostatic/bin

./test_3d_ophelie_boussinesq_stratified_hydrostatic --mode=stratified --dp=0.025
./test_3d_ophelie_boussinesq_stratified_hydrostatic --mode=uniform --dp=0.025
./test_3d_ophelie_boussinesq_stratified_hydrostatic --mode=stratified --dp=0.02
```

## 2. Doc77-B — 1D conduction

**Case:** `test_3d_ophelie_thermal_1d_conduction`  
**Reuse path:** `OpheliePairwiseLaplaceCK` + `ApplyLaplaceDiffusionDtCK` (ΔT-authoritative; T0=1473 K)  
**Analytic:** Tss + A sin(πx/L) exp(−απ²t/L²); end Dirichlet Th/Tc  
**Logs:** [`reference/doc77b_runs/`](reference/doc77b_runs/)

| Run | decay_ok | amp_ok | l2_ok | linf_ok | amp_rel | l2_rel | passed |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| dp=0.02, t=6 s | 1 | 1 | 1 | 1 | 0.046 | 0.047 | **1** |
| dp=0.015, t=6 s | 1 | 1 | 1 | 1 | 0.038 | 0.056 | **1** |

Smoke note: default `k=200` (elevated vs glass k=4) so modal time τ≈60 s is reachable in a short run; path is still production ΔT/Laplace. Amp error improves with refine; L2_rel slightly worse at finer dp on this short window (boundary-shell / 3D lateral effects) — longer-time / thinner-slab check optional.

### Commands

```bash
BIN=build/tests/extra_source_and_tests/3d_examples/test_3d_ophelie_thermal_1d_conduction/bin
cd "$BIN"
./test_3d_ophelie_thermal_1d_conduction --dp=0.02 --end-time=6 --dt=0.1
./test_3d_ophelie_thermal_1d_conduction --dp=0.015 --end-time=6 --dt=0.05
```

**Verdict B:** **PASS** on dp=0.02 short window (L2_rel≈0.047, amp_rel≈0.046, mode decays).
## 3. Doc77-C / French cylinder

**Doc77-C:** circulation **PASS**; Nu≤3% **OPEN** (~8% best) — [Doc79](79_DOC77C_DEVAHL_DAVIS_ENTRY_2026-10-01.md).
